00982668	When adapting the limit values to the machining, usually trial runs of the machining processes are carried out to test the response of the monitoring system to the various load situations.<p>During these trial runs, it is often expedient to prevent the not-yet optimized monitoring system from intervening in the machine processes. The setup function enables the triggered alarms to be shown on the display WITHOUT any effect on the machine controller.<p>However, depending on the settings made when the system was commissioned, one limit of the Overload type at the top limit of the measuring range to monitor the machine for collisions may remain active when the Adjust mode is running.<p><b>Application of this function suppresses transmission of all alarms (with the possible exception of collision alarms, see below) from the monitoring system to the PLC.<p><b>Damage to workpiece, tool or machine will not be minimized as long as the setup function remains active.<p><b>You should start this function only when you are sure that you wish to suppress transmission of the alarms to the PLC while making settings on the monitoring system.

00983528	The teach-in function enables you to determine the monitor signals of a normal, fault-free machining operation as comparison values (100% values).<p>The monitor signals for the subsequent machining operations are then compared with these values. Deviations of the current monitor signals from the comparison values will then result in alarm outputs from the monitoring system, depending on the active limit types and limit settings.<p>A teach-in process is suggested after changing a workpiece batch or the machining program, if the monitor signals show clear changes compared to the past machining processes and/or if it comes to wrong alarms.<p><b>Make sure that the parameters for the teach-in meet your requirements before starting the teach-in process.<p><b>Use only tools of operational sharpness for the teach-in! Always start the teach-in process when machining a new workpiece!

009842d0	False alarms can happen during idle passes while reworking workpieces that have been machined in part with the same program.
This is valid only for limits of the <b>Underload, Missing, Work-under, Cut Start, Cut End</b> and <b>Signal Pattern lower</b> types.
The <b>REWORK</b> function can be started to avoid false alarms.
This is also recommended when the monitoring system has interrupted the machining process with an alarm.<p><b>Note: Application of this function while a machining process is running will result in the suppression of all alarms for limits of the underload, missing, work under, Through rising, Through falling and Signal pattern lower types until the function is ended by the operator.<p><b>Damage to workpiece, tool or machine will not be minimized as long as the function remains active!<p><b>Start this function only when you are sure that you wish to suppress the alarms until the function is terminated!
